<TD NOWRAP VALIGN="bottom">&nbsp;</TD>

<TD NOWRAP VALIGN="bottom">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD NOWRAP VALIGN="bottom" ALIGN="right">36</TD>
<TD NOWRAP VALIGN="bottom">%&nbsp;</TD></TR>
</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">In US$ million, unless noted otherwise. EBITDA = Operating income + Depreciation of PP&amp;E + Depreciation of the right of
use assets + Amortization of intangible assets + Unproductive exploratory drillings + (Reversal) / Deterioration of PP&amp;E. Adjusted EBITDA = EBITDA that excludes IFRS 16 effects +/- <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">one-off</FONT> items. Net
Leverage Ratio = Net Debt / LTM Adj. EBITDA. FCF = Cash flow from Operations less CAPEX (Investing activities), M&amp;A (Investing activities), and interest and leasing payments (Financing activities). Fuels = diesel + gasoline. R&amp;M is refining
and marketing business, it excludes petrochemicals and agro products. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Adjusted EBITDA</B> totaled US$1.2&nbsp;billion
<FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-3%</FONT> vs. 1Q24), on the back of increasing costs in dollar terms, reflecting the catch up with the devaluation of <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">mid-December</FONT> (though lower replacement cost of our
inventories in 1Q24), and reduced conventional output, affected by severe weather conditions in the south of Argentina in June. These were partially offset by higher seasonal gas, better fuel prices, growing oil exports and tariff update at
Metrogas. Interannually, it expanded 20%, mostly due to higher fuel prices (though lower demand) and hydrocarbon output growth, partially offset by increased cost due to higher activity. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Hydrocarbon production</B> averaged 539 kboe/d (+2% q/q and +5% y/y), boosted by shale operations, representing 52% of the total. <B>Crude oil</B> declined
3% q/q, since conventional production was affected by harsh weather conditions, while recording a growth of 3% y/y, driven by steady <B>shale oil</B> positive contribution, reaching 113 kbbl/d in 2Q24.<B> Medanito oil exports to Chile </B>averaged
29 kbbl/d (+25% q/q). </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Domestic fuel demand </B>declined by 2% q/q, mainly due to gasoline demand contraction, mostly offset by higher seasonal diesel
demand, addressed with inventory drawdowns, thus avoiding imports. <B>Processing levels at our refineries</B> averaged 299 kbbl/d, reaching a refining utilization rate of 91%. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>CAPEX</B><SUP STYLE="font-size:75%; vertical-align:top">2</SUP><B> </B>totaled US$1.2&nbsp;billion (+3% q/q), on track to fully deploy the investment plan
of the year. Over 70% of the total capex was concentrated in the upstream segment, mainly shale operations, in line with the Company&#146;s strategy to grow. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Free cash flow</B> totaled a negative US$257&nbsp;million, compared to a negative US$394&nbsp;million in 1Q24. Both periods were affected by payments of
imports deferred from 2023, being more pronounced in 1Q24. Although higher seasonal sales pressured liquidity in 2Q24, collections from certain gas clients deferred from 1Q24, plus dividend collection from affiliates improved free cash flow. Since
gross debt was steady, <B>net debt</B> amounted to US$7.5&nbsp;billion (vs. US$7.2&nbsp;billion in 1Q24), while maintaining <B>net leverage ratio</B> at 1.7x. </P>
<P STYLE="line-height:8.0pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:2pt;border-bottom:1px solid #000000;width:11%">&nbsp;</P>

<FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">Jun-2024</FONT> (51% Argentina Government; 35% NYSE and 14% ByMA). </P></TD></TR></TABLE>

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Net Revenues</B> amounted to US$4,935&nbsp;million (+15% q/q), mainly driven by higher seasonal sales of natural gas (as
producer and our affiliate Metrogas, as distributor, boosted by tariff update in April), agricultural products (grain and flour exports and fertilizers in the local market), combined with increased local diesel demand, better fuel prices and growing
oil exports. All of them, partially offset by gasoline demand contraction. </P> <P STYLE="font-size:12pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Stock variations include price effects by (US$36) million in 2Q23, (US$169) million for 1Q24, US$12 million for 2Q24, (US$65)
million in 1H23 and (US$157) million in 1H24. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>OPEX</B> totaled US$1,842&nbsp;million (+33% q/q), mostly explained by higher costs in dollars terms
across all business segments, reflecting the <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">catch-up</FONT> with December&#146;s devaluation, in addition to increases related to the maintenance of our refineries.<B> </B><B>Other costs</B> totaled
US$383&nbsp;million (+41% q/q), primarily due to higher taxes related to increased exports, in addition to the results of Argerich, the first offshore ultra deep-water well in Argentina<SUP STYLE="font-size:75%; vertical-align:top">3</SUP>. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Total Purchases and Stock Variations</B> amounted to US$1,188&nbsp;million (+9% q/q), mainly due to higher seasonal purchases of agro products and natural
gas (Metrogas), and crude oil to third parties (due to lower own production and higher local price). However, gasoline demand contraction and diesel inventories drawdown avoided fuel imports in 2Q24. Also, stock variation was negative in 2Q24 <FONT
STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-US$3</FONT> million), but not as pronounced as 1Q24 <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-US$124</FONT> million), which recorded lower replacement cost of inventories. </P>

<TD WIDTH="4%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left"><SUP STYLE="font-size:75%; vertical-align:top">3</SUP>&nbsp;</TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman; " ALIGN="left">For more information, please refer to &#147;Exploratory Strategy&#148; in section 4.1. </P></TD></TR></TABLE>
 <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman" ALIGN="right">Page 3/13 </P>

<p style="margin-top:1em; margin-bottom:0em; page-break-before:always"> </p>
<HR SIZE="3" style="COLOR:#999999" WIDTH="100%" ALIGN="CENTER">


<Center><DIV STYLE="width:8.5in" align="left">

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">


<IMG SRC="g884730g01s05.jpg" ALT="LOGO">
 </P> <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P>
 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Net income before tax</B> reached US$456&nbsp;million <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-15%</FONT> q/q),
as a result of reduced equity income and operating income, partially offset by better net financial results, driven by higher gains from holding financial securities. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">The <B>income tax</B> was positive at US$79&nbsp;million in 2Q24 (vs. US$121&nbsp;million positive in 1Q24), mainly due to lower deferred tax liabilities, as
inflation outpaced devaluation in both periods. Thus, the <B>net income</B> amounted to US$535&nbsp;million <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-19%</FONT> q/q). </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:18pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>3. ADJ. EBITDA&nbsp;&amp; CAPEX </B></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>3.1 ADJ. EBITDA
RECONCILIATION </B></P> <P STYLE="font-size:12pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>

<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>CAPEX</B> new definition: during 2Q24, YPF revised the criteria of defining &#147;Investments&#148;. Previously, total PP&amp;E acquisitions, net of well
abandonment cost, were reported as the best approximation of &#147;Investments&#148;. Under the new criteria, we included acquisitions of intangible assets and excluded consumption of the period in the Operating Costs category, among other
adjustments. Likewise, the information for comparative periods was <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">re-expressed.</FONT> It is worth mentioning that this new definition does not alter accounting standards of the Company. The amounts of the <FONT
STYLE="white-space:nowrap">re-expressed</FONT> &#147;Investments&#148; are shown below: </P> <P STYLE="font-size:12pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Revenues</B> totaled almost US$2.2&nbsp;billion in 2Q24 (+9% q/q), mainly driven by higher seasonal gas sales (+35% price
and +7% volume), and to a minor extent, better crude oil price (+4%), both in local and international markets, partially offset by lower crude oil production <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-3%),</FONT> particularly conventional output, which was
affected by severe winter weather in the south of Argentina in June. </P>
 <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman" ALIGN="right">Page 5/13 </P>

</DIV></Center>


<p style="margin-top:1em; margin-bottom:0em; page-break-before:always"> </p>
<HR SIZE="3" style="COLOR:#999999" WIDTH="100%" ALIGN="CENTER">


<Center><DIV STYLE="width:8.5in" align="left">

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">


<IMG SRC="g884730g01s05.jpg" ALT="LOGO">
 </P> <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P>
 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Lifting cost</B> reached US$16.2/BOE (+25% q/q), primarily due to higher costs in dollar terms after
December&#146;s devaluation (pulling, maintenance, among others), coupled with lower conventional oil output: <B>conventional </B>at US$28.7/BOE (+33% q/q) and <B>unconventional </B>at US$5.7/BOE (+23% q/q). Zooming in, lifting cost of our <B>shale
core hub</B> blocks, on a gross basis (at 100% stake), also increased to US$4.7/BOE (+42% q/q), mainly due to cost <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">catch-up</FONT> with devaluation, mentioned before, and specific higher pulling and maintenance
costs. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Other costs</B> totaled US$283&nbsp;million (+22% q/q), primarily due to cost <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">catch-up</FONT> mentioned
before, in addition to the results of Argerich, the first offshore ultra deep-water well in Argentina<SUP STYLE="font-size:75%; vertical-align:top">4</SUP>. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Royalties </B>and other taxes averaged US$7.2/BOE (+7% q/q), mostly as a result of higher natural gas prices. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Adjusted EBITDA</B> totaled US$822&nbsp;million <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-2%</FONT> q/q), driven by increased costs after December&#146;s
devaluation and reduced conventional oil, mostly offset by higher seasonal sales of natural gas and better oil prices. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>CAPEX</B> amounted to
US$863&nbsp;million <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-8%</FONT> q/q), mainly because mature fields are recategorized as assets held for sale, partially offset by higher unconventional activities, mainly allocated to drilling and workover
activities. More than 85% of the segment&#146;s capex was deployed within the unconventional areas. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Drilling and workover activities</B> grew
significantly, drilling a total of 79 wells in our operated blocks: 58 horizontal shale (100% oil) and 21 conventional. In terms of <B>efficiencies within our shale operations</B>, during 2Q24 the Company continued achieving remarkable results in
drilling and fracking performance, averaging 292 meters per day in drilling and 237 stages per set per month on fracking, increasing by 29% and 22%, respectively vs. 2Q23. Moreover, during June, we achieved the highest lateral length drilling speed
for one well in La Angostura Sur block, reaching over 1,500 meters in a single day. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Regarding our <B>exploratory strategy</B>, we made progress in the
following areas: </P> <P STYLE="font-size:6pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">
<TR style = "page-break-inside:avoid">
<TD WIDTH="1%">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="2%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left">&#149;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="1%" VALIGN="top">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P ALIGN="left" STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt"><B></B>South of <B>Mendoza Province</B>, expanding the frontiers of our
<FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">Core-Hub</FONT> in Vaca Muerta: we drilled two wells in May and gathered promising oil productivity data </P></TD></TR></TABLE> <P STYLE="font-size:6pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">

Argentina: we completed the first horizontal well at El Cerrito Block in June </P></TD></TR></TABLE> <P STYLE="font-size:6pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>

Argentina, at <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">CAN-100</FONT> block. Although the result was negative, it allowed us to gather relevant information to continue exploring the hydrocarbon potential of the Argentinean Sea. It is worth mentioning that
the result of Argerich affects only a portion of the <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">CAN-100&#146;s</FONT> prospectivity. </P></TD></TR></TABLE> <P STYLE="font-size:12pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Crude oil production</B> in 2Q24 continued at strong levels, averaging 249 kbbl/d, driven by shale growth (+1% q/q), which
slightly offset the decrease in the conventional output <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-6%</FONT> q/q), constrained by the extreme winter conditions during June in the south of Argentina. Additionally, 9% of conventional output came from tertiary
production, growing by 6% y/y and minimizing the natural decline in mature fields. </P> <P STYLE="line-height:8.0pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:2pt;border-bottom:1px solid #000000;width:11%">&nbsp;</P>

</P></TD></TR></TABLE>

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Natural gas production </B>increased by 7% q/q, mainly due to winter peak season. <B>NGLs production</B>
also grew by 10% q/q, mainly driven by the new turboexpander in Loma La Lata block, enhancing NGLs recovery level. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>4.2 DOWNSTREAM </B></P>

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Stock variations include price effects by (US$31) million in 2Q23, US$102&nbsp;million for 1Q24, US$34&nbsp;million for 2Q24,
(US$90) million for 1H23 and US$136&nbsp;million for 1H24. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Revenues</B> totaled almost US$4&nbsp;billion in 2Q24 (+5% q/q), mainly boosted by higher
local sales of diesel and fertilizers, and increased exports of grain and flour, and crude oil, partially offset by demand drop of gasoline. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Refining
and logistics costs </B>increased by 20% q/q, primarily driven by an in increase in costs in real terms, reflecting the catch up with December&#146;s devaluation, in addition to higher activities related to programmed shutdown and maintenance. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>No fuel imports</B> were recorded in 2Q24, on the back of gasoline demand contraction, coupled with a record high production of gasoline at La Plata
refinery, and inventories reduction to tackle increase in diesel demand. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Crude oil purchases</B> (including intersegment) grew by 5% q/q, mostly on
the back of higher local oil price. Whereas <B>biofuel purchases</B> fell 7% q/q, mainly due to lower price<SUP STYLE="font-size:75%; vertical-align:top">5</SUP> and volume of biodiesel, coupled with lower purchased volume of bioethanol, partially
offset by higher price of bioethanol. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Adjusted EBITDA,</B> excluding inventories price effect of oil products, totaled US$369&nbsp;million <FONT
STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-35%</FONT> q/q), mainly explained by the cost <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">catch-up</FONT> with December&#146;s devaluation, in addition to higher cost of crude oil (pricing), and gasoline demand contraction. These
effects were partially offset by diesel demand growth, better fuel prices in the local market, no import of fuels and increasing oil exports to Chile. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Adjusted EBITDA of the Refining</B><B></B><B>&nbsp;&amp; Marketing</B> business, in unit terms, reached US$12.7/bbl in 2Q24, compared to US$19.1/bbl
reported in 1Q24 and US$10.3/bbl in 2Q23. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>CAPEX</B> totaled US$299&nbsp;million (+52% q/q), mainly growing in midstream oil (Vaca Muerta South) and
refining (to reduce sulfur content in fuels at La Plata refinery. The breakdown of total capex is as follows: 51% refining, 33% midstream oil, 13% logistics and 3% commercial. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">In our <B>refineries,</B> during 2Q24 CAPEX was allocated in the following main projects: </P>
<P STYLE="font-size:6pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">
<TR style = "page-break-inside:avoid">
<TD WIDTH="1%">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="2%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left">&#149;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="1%" VALIGN="top">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P ALIGN="left" STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt"><B>New fuel specifications</B> project including the construction of a new diesel oil hydrotreatment unit at
Luj&aacute;n de Cuyo refinery, expected to be operational by 4Q25, as well as the new gasoline hydrotreatment plant and the revamping completion of existing gasoline units at La Plata refinery, that became operational in 2Q24. The main purpose of
these works is to comply with the new fuel specifications established by Resolution No.&nbsp;492/2023 of the Secretary of Energy. </P></TD></TR></TABLE>
<P STYLE="line-height:8.0pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:2pt;border-bottom:1px solid #000000;width:11%">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">
<TR style = "page-break-inside:avoid">
<TD WIDTH="4%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left"><SUP STYLE="font-size:75%; vertical-align:top">5</SUP>&nbsp;</TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman; " ALIGN="left">During May, the Secretary of Energy decreased regulated price in AR$ for biodiesel, while bioethanol increased.
In June, both of them were increased. </P></TD></TR></TABLE>

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; margin-left:2%; text-indent:-2%; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Net Average domestic prices for gasoline and diesel are net of taxes, commissions, commercial
bonuses and freights. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Crude oil processed </B>stood at 299 Kbbl/d in 2Q24 remaining almost flat sequentially
<FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-1%),</FONT> and decreasing 2% interannually, mainly due to limited availability at La Plata refinery, which was affected by a stoppage, adverse weather conditions and an inconvenience in the Puerto Rosales &#150;
La Plata pipeline for a few days, which was already restored. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Domestic fuel sales volumes </B>decreased by 2% q/q, driven by an 11% drop in gasoline
demand, especially in premium retail, partially offset by a 5% increase in diesel demand, especially derived from seasonality and higher sales to industrial segment. Our market share amounted to 56% in gasoline and 55% in diesel. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Petrochemicals volumes</B> stood almost flat <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-2%</FONT> q/q), primarily as a result of lower methanol exports and
stoppage in the plant during June, partially offset by higher methanol demand in the local market. </P>
 <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman" ALIGN="right">Page 8/13 </P>

</DIV></Center>


<p style="margin-top:1em; margin-bottom:0em; page-break-before:always"> </p>
<HR SIZE="3" style="COLOR:#999999" WIDTH="100%" ALIGN="CENTER">


<Center><DIV STYLE="width:8.5in" align="left">

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">


<IMG SRC="g884730g01s05.jpg" ALT="LOGO">
 </P> <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P>
 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Fertilizers, grain and flours sales volumes</B> expanded by 135% q/q, mainly boosted by higher seasonal
demand, particularly the export of grain and flours. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Net average fuels prices in the domestic market measured in dollar terms </B>increased by 3% q/q
and 14% y/y, derived from price adjustments to mitigate devaluation impact and narrow the gap to import parity, which decreased to 5% in 2Q24, from 7% in 1Q24 and 13% in 2Q23. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Prices for petrochemical</B><B></B><B>&nbsp;&amp; other refined products</B> increased by 3% q/q, aligned with an upward trend in international prices of
certain products such as methanol, virgin naphtha and fuel oil. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>4.3 GAS&nbsp;&amp; POWER </B></P>

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Revenues</B> totaled US$862&nbsp;million in 2Q24 (+79% q/q), mainly due to seasonal natural gas sales, as producer and
Metrogas, our affiliate, as distributor, coupled with tariffs adjustments at Metrogas in last April. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Natural gas purchases</B> grew by 60% q/q, in
line with higher seasonal natural gas sales, while <B>operating costs</B><B></B><B>&nbsp;&amp; other</B> increased by 51%, mostly driven by increasing costs in dollar terms. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Moreover, according to Resolution N&deg; 58/24 of the Secretary of Energy, released on May&nbsp;8, 2024, the Company recognized a charge for doubtful sales
receivables within operating costs of US$22&nbsp;million in 2Q24 and US$29&nbsp;million in 1Q24, mainly owed by CAMMESA. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">As a result of the
aforementioned factors, <B>Adjusted EBITDA</B> totaled a positive US$59&nbsp;million, compared to a negative US$30&nbsp;million in 1Q24. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>CAPEX</B>
totaled US$18&nbsp;million (+35% q/q) and continued focusing on the following projects: </P> <P STYLE="font-size:6pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">FCF = Cash flow from Operations less capex (Investing activities), M&amp;A (Investing activities), and interest and leasing
payments (Financing activities). </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Net cash flow from operations, coupled with investing activities</B>, totaled a negative US$42&nbsp;million in 2Q24.
Although adjusted EBITDA was similar to capex in 2Q24, it was affected by higher working capital pressure, such as increased seasonal sales of gas, payment of 2023&#146;s imports deferred to 2Q24 (but collected from certain gas clients the payment
delayed from 1Q24) and higher purchases of crude oil to third parties due to lower conventional production. All of them were partially offset by dividend collection from affiliates. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Net cash flow from financing activities</B> amounted to a negative US$206&nbsp;million in 2Q24, mostly driven by the final amortization of 2024&#146;s
international bond paid at maturity (US$ 208 million), in addition to regular debt services, partially offset by the issuance of a local hard dollar bond (US$ 178 million). Moreover, the Company continued securing trade facilities and other loans.
</P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">As a result, the <B>free cash flow</B> for the period was negative at U$257&nbsp;million, as our investing activities and our regular interest payments
were not fully compensated by the cash flow from operations. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">In terms of liquidity, our <B>cash and short-term investments</B> declined to
US$1,394&nbsp;million by the end of June 2024, contracting 13% vs. 1Q24. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>5.2 NET DEBT </B></P>

<HR SIZE="3" style="COLOR:#999999" WIDTH="100%" ALIGN="CENTER">


<Center><DIV STYLE="width:8.5in" align="left">

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">


<IMG SRC="g884730g01s05.jpg" ALT="LOGO">
 </P> <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P>
 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">As of June&nbsp;30, 2024, YPF&#146;s <B>consolidated net debt</B> totaled US$7,457&nbsp;million
(+US$257&nbsp;million vs. 1Q24). Despite higher net debt compared to the previous quarter, the Company managed to maintain <B>net leverage ratio</B> around 1.7x. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">In terms of <B>financing</B>, during 2Q24, the Company issued a <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">2-year</FONT>
<FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">hard-US$</FONT> local bond for US$178&nbsp;million at an yield of 6%. On the other hand, the Company paid at maturity US$60&nbsp;million quarterly amortization of the Notes 2026 and the remaining US$208&nbsp;million
of the Notes 2024. After 2Q24, the Company issued a US$-link bond for US$185&nbsp;million at a 0% yield, with a <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">two-year</FONT> maturity, and promissory notes for US$100&nbsp;million at a 0% yield, with maturities of
up to 18 months. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Regarding our <B>maturity profile</B>, the Company faces debt maturities for the remaining 6 months of 2024 for US$854&nbsp;million,
mainly concentrated in short-term trade facilities for almost US$400&nbsp;million and international bonds&#146; amortizations. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">The following chart shows
our consolidated principal debt maturity profile as of June&nbsp;30, 2024: </P> <P STYLE="font-size:12pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P> <P STYLE="font-size:0pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt" ALIGN="center">


<IMG SRC="g884730g12s04.jpg" ALT="LOGO">
 </P>
 <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman" ALIGN="right">Page 11/13 </P>

<TD NOWRAP VALIGN="bottom"><I>%&nbsp;</I></TD></TR></TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Note: Information reported in accordance with
International Financial Reporting Standards (IFRS). </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>7. ABOUT YPF </B></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:6pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">YPF is the largest energy company in Argentina, fully integrated in the oil and gas value chain. Our main businesses are: (i)&nbsp;in the upstream, we produce
roughly 35% and 30% of the country&#146;s crude oil and natural gas, respectively; (ii)&nbsp;in the downstream, we operate 3 refineries representing approximately 50% of Argentina&#146;s refining capacity and lead the local diesel and gasoline sales
with a market share of more than 55%; and (iii)&nbsp;in gas and power, Metrogas, our subsidiary, distributes around 25% of the country&#146;s natural gas, while YPF Luz, our affiliate, is the third largest power generation company in Argentina. The
Government is the controlling shareholder with a 51% stake, and YPF is listed in the NYSE and ByMA. </P> <P STYLE="font-size:18pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
